# AI Media Scout — 2026-11-12

Today’s open-ended creative-AI edition contains 66 detailed guides and 66 additional screened discoveries, including completion of earlier pending guides. New fields cover learned optical holography and generative literature; additional work spans real-footage editing, voice/music, fashion references, interactive installation, CAD and creative electronics. All platforms are represented according to documented support, with unsupported requirements left unknown. All selected software grants were read in full; model weights, paid hosts/services and artwork permissions remain separate. Quality labels reflect six dated evidence checks: a complete guide, activity or stars do not grant Recommended/Ready to try, and only current cited prototype/unfinished-work reasons justify Experimental. These are source reviews; no discovered application was installed or run. The existing weekly pass is complete through yesterday’s 2,484 tools; today’s new/expanded records have current source assessments and the final whole-library coverage gate will be checked. Primary research spans GitHub and all eight configured external ecosystems, with live context for all 100 fields. Noncommercial grants and unresolved license/provenance cases are excluded or watchlisted. Raw unreviewed candidates stay local. Search completion does not mean every returned candidate has been reviewed, nor that discovery is exhaustive. Verified collection: 861/861 repository queries attempted; 861 successful, 0 partial, 0 failed and 91 bounded/truncated. All 25 model tasks were attempted; 1 failed. These are bounded discovery attempts, not an exhaustive census or quality approval. Empty model-source responses: text2text-generation. An empty response does not establish that these creative workflows have no tools. Model-source gap (text-to-image): Source response exceeded the 2 MB collection limit. Primary web research supplements the field; the failed collector response remains a disclosed gap. Separate from successful search queries, 45 watchlist fetches and 5 enrichment attempts failed; the detailed warnings remain in the report. A 404/409 is an availability gap, not proof of abandonment or license revocation.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

## Coverage

| Images & design | Invoke’s current primary site describes a layered generation canvas. Today’s Infinite Canvas and local inspiration-library guides distinguish the interface, chosen inference provider and model/asset permissions. [Source 1](https://invoke.ai/) |
| Video, animation & film | OpenReelio and Kaestral describe agent-assisted editing of actual footage. Phosphene’s current changelog advertises 5.0.19 but also retains older highlighted release text; its Pro features must not be attributed to the free route. The existing full guide is preserved; the current release has not been reviewed into a new guide. [Source 1](https://openreelio.com/) [Source 2](https://www.kaestral.com/) [Source 3](https://phosphene.video/changelog/) |

MusicStudio’s separate SongBench academic restriction is excluded from its assessed generation/mastering route. | AI seamless loops, sample design and DAW-ready stems | WAIvePulse’s site describes song generation, mixing, looping and performance on local hardware. Today’s Attic, Cochlea and Sonic Bridge guides distinguish learned generation, external-agent composition and deterministic signal descriptors. [Source 1](https://waivepulse.com/) |
| Neural yarn modeling and machine knitting | The primary inverse-knitting paper describes recovering procedural yarn structure from images. A neural yarn representation is not automatically a machine-knit instruction file. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.00154) |
| Neural watermarking for printed creative media | StegaStamp’s author site describes learned links embedded in physical photographs. Printed robustness, decoder deployment and source/model terms require separate review. [Source 1](https://www.matthewtancik.com/stegastamp) |
| Learned halftones and neural print screening | The author’s Deep Halftoning page describes learned descreening components; a CVF paper describes reversible neural halftones. One attempted full PDF remained unreadable after a bounded capture. Neither paper availability nor a model claim establishes a complete software grant. [Source 1](https://nikronic.com/projects/deep-halftoning_project/) [Source 2](https://ttwong12.github.io/papers/invhalftone/invhalftone.pdf) [Source 3](https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/ICCV2021/html/Xia_Deep_Halftoning_With_Reversible_Binary_Pattern_ICCV_2021_paper.html) |
| AI brick sculpture and agent-assisted construction design | BrickGPT and BrickWright author pages distinguish trained text-to-brick generation from a coding-agent toolkit for assembling and checking brick builds. Their current sites are context; no unchanged previously published guide is relisted. [Source 1](https://avalovelace1.github.io/BrickGPT/) [Source 2](https://avalovelace1.github.io/brickwright/) |
| Neural optical holography and computational displays | New neural-holography searches include learned SLM phase generation and calibrated display transport. Stanford Neural Holography and UNC DeepCGH have non-commercial code terms; AXCGH’s root MIT does not resolve its upstream provenance. Odak’s actual learned light-transport components receive a scoped open-source guide. [Source 1](https://www.computationalimaging.org/publications/neuralholography/) [Source 2](https://www.computationalimaging.org/publications/time-multiplexed-neural-holography/) [Source 3](https://light.princeton.edu/publication/artifact-resilient-holography/) [Source 4](https://www.kaanaksit.com/odak/odak/learn_models/) [Source 5](https://www.kaanaksit.com/odak/installation/) [Source 6](https://www.kaanaksit.com/odak/cgh/) [Source 7](https://cinescope-wkr.github.io/pado-hologram/) |
| AI poetry, kinetic literature and language installations | New searches cover neural poetry, language installations and typographic performance. RiTa explicitly calls its current tools AI-free and is not admitted merely as computational writing. J-Space Theater’s real Qwen mode and Sympoiesis’s LLM videopoem workflow have separate guides; Jhave’s site yielded no usable text. [Source 1](https://rednoise.org/rita/) [Source 2](https://jhave.com/) [Source 3](https://github.com/Cybefutu/j-space-theater) |

## Search and review counts

100 creative fields; 861/861 repository queries attempted; 25/25 model-task queries attempted. 51239 distinct source candidates and 1023 model leads. 66 detailed profiles and 66 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 91 bounded repository queries, 1 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: Fresh GitLab raw AudioStellar, embedded-amplifier, terrain, JoyAI MLX and Openedit documentation, plus a current video-editor topic listing, were inspected. JoyAI/Openedit are already retained entries, not new repeated profiles. Xotion’s complete PolyForm Noncommercial text remains excluded. The topic list also contains conventional non-AI editors; this bounded pass is not all of GitLab. [Source](https://gitlab.com/ayrsd/audiostellar/-/raw/audiostellar2/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/algomus.fr/perso-ai/-/raw/master/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/libreterrain/libreterrain/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/ar39907/embedded-amp-model/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/LICENSE) [Source](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/ahkamboh/xotion-studio/-/raw/main/LICENSE) [Source](https://gitlab.com/explore/projects/topics/video-editor)
- **codeberg** · searched: Ordinary public reads returned real Imaginer/Raspberry-Pi-Diffusion project content and actual Imaginer API metadata, not a bot-check page. Broader diffusion/audio repository search endpoints returned 429, and search-service host restrictions also limited discovery. No new complete software-license/hardware clearance is inferred from project metadata. [Source](https://codeberg.org/Imaginer/Imaginer) [Source](https://codeberg.org/MightyOwlbear/Raspberry-Pi-5-Diffusion) [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/Imaginer/Imaginer) [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/search?q=diffusion&limit=25) [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/search?q=audio&limit=25)
- **sourcehut** · searched: The current public project search returned diffront, diffuser and prs-search. Fresh diffuser Cargo.toml and complete MIT text were inspected; its author-local diffusers-rs path remains a reproducibility gap. The retained project is not republished unchanged, and the host search result is not a full grant for the additional candidates. [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/Cargo.toml) [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source](https://sr.ht/projects?search=diffusion)
- **packages** · searched: Live npm LiteRT OCR, PyPI NorthCel/game-localizer/gameloc and NuGet TensorStack.Media records were inspected, along with versioned Brandly/manimvox metadata. Registry tags are discovery evidence, not complete software-license or quality review. A pinned version URL is not presented as the latest package; NorthCel’s live response reports 0.7.1/Python >=3.10. [Source](https://api.nuget.org/v3-flatcontainer/tensorstack.media/index.json) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/manimvox/0.2.0/json) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/brandly-cli/0.3.11/json) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/game-localizer/json) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/gameloc/json) [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/litert-ocr/latest) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/northcel/json)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: Current Krita AI and Blender denoising documentation supplement fresh full-license/source review of ComfyUI QwenVL, EffectCraft, Paint a Plate and the After Effects adapter. The separate IRCAM nn~ code is non-commercial and is excluded. Actual learned analysis/roto or agent composition operations are distinguished from conventional rendering. Adobe host, plug-in code, model weights and optional commercial renderer terms stay separate. [Source](https://docs.interstice.cloud/) [Source](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/render/cycles/render_settings/sampling.html) [Source](https://github.com/1038lab/ComfyUI-QwenVL) [Source](https://github.com/storytold/effectcraft) [Source](https://github.com/dcc-mcp/dcc-mcp-aftereffects)
- **project-sites** · searched: Fresh primary captures cover all 100 observed fields, including optical holography and generative literature. Some URLs failed, some pages were empty shells, four PDFs were bounded/unreadable, and the large Odak model-documentation HTML was bounded; its specific neural source file was read instead. Current release marketing is not a substitute for source/model terms or independent results. [Source](https://www.kaestral.com/) [Source](https://www.kerfline.build/) [Source](https://phosphene.video/changelog/) [Source](https://laplacianai.github.io/manimvox/) [Source](https://suite.sat.qc.ca/en/tools/) [Source](https://sat-mtl.github.io/koaia/) [Source](https://sat-mtl.github.io/livepose/) [Source](https://www.computationalimaging.org/publications/neuralholography/) [Source](https://www.computationalimaging.org/publications/time-multiplexed-neural-holography/) [Source](https://light.princeton.edu/publication/artifact-resilient-holography/) [Source](https://rednoise.org/rita/) [Source](https://jhave.com/)
- **research-code** · searched: Stanford holography, UNC DeepCGH and HoloTorch received complete software-license checks: their non-commercial terms exclude those codebases. AXCGH provenance remains unresolved. Odak’s complete MPL-2.0 grant and actual learned light-transport source support a scoped new guide; generic differentiable optics are not automatically AI. [Source](https://www.computationalimaging.org/publications/neuralholography/) [Source](https://www.computationalimaging.org/publications/time-multiplexed-neural-holography/) [Source](https://light.princeton.edu/publication/artifact-resilient-holography/) [Source](https://github.com/UNC-optics/DeepCGH/blob/master/copyright.pdf) [Source](https://github.com/kaanaksit/odak/blob/master/odak/learn/models/models.py)
- **international** · searched: Chinese fashion/vision/CAD sources, Japanese Fullseye MCP documentation, Dutch/English Patchkamer material, Arabic Taarib documentation and multilingual music/poetry workflows were researched. Language and research origin do not determine tier; local/remote inference, exact model permissions and setup gaps remain workflow-specific. ## Voicebox

Audio, music & voice · Performance, projection & stage media · Avatars, digital humans & lip sync · Accessible media & assistive creation

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Draft narration, character dialogue and podcast scenes using voices you have permission to use; audition takes and add post-production effects.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-12. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/jamiepine/voicebox/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The official README links a voicebox.sh demonstration and screenshots of the voice and Stories editors. The developer describes eight TTS engines; engine speed and quality claims have not been independently reproduced here. [Source](https://github.com/jamiepine/voicebox/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://voicebox.sh)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use the official Apple Silicon/Intel macOS DMG or Windows MSI. Linux currently needs the documented source/backend route. Developers need Bun, Rust, Python 3.12, just, Tauri prerequisites and Xcode on macOS; use just setup then just dev in the checkout. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/jamiepine/voicebox/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://voicebox.sh)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** The MIT application license does not license Qwen, LuxTTS, Chatterbox, TADA, Kokoro or Whisper weights collectively. Review each selected engine/model card and reference-voice rights; models are separate downloads. Application code is MIT; local hardware and model storage are required. Optional external clients and any selected service have separate terms. [Source](https://github.com/jamiepine/voicebox/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://voicebox.sh)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Only Chatterbox Turbo interprets the documented expressive tags; other engines may speak them literally. Windows/Linux automatic paste parity remains on the roadmap. A local UI does not make every model equally fast or prove every integration is private.

### How it uses AI

A local voice workstation combines neural speech synthesis, reference-voice cloning, Whisper transcription and a local Qwen language model with an editable multivoice story timeline. [Source](https://github.com/jamiepine/voicebox/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://voicebox.sh)

### Introduction

A local voice workstation combines neural speech synthesis, reference-voice cloning, Whisper transcription and a local Qwen language model with an editable multivoice story timeline. [Source 1](https://github.com/jamiepine/voicebox/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://voicebox.sh)

### What it is good for

Draft narration, character dialogue and podcast scenes using voices you have permission to use; audition takes and add post-production effects. [Source 1](https://github.com/jamiepine/voicebox/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://voicebox.sh)

### Demo & examples

The official README links a voicebox.sh demonstration and screenshots of the voice and Stories editors. The developer describes eight TTS engines; engine speed and quality claims have not been independently reproduced here. [Source 1](https://github.com/jamiepine/voicebox/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://voicebox.sh)

### Install

Use the official Apple Silicon/Intel macOS DMG or Windows MSI. Linux currently needs the documented source/backend route. Developers need Bun, Rust, Python 3.12, just, Tauri prerequisites and Xcode on macOS; use just setup then just dev in the checkout. [Source 1](https://github.com/jamiepine/voicebox/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://voicebox.sh)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the official Apple Silicon/Intel macOS DMG or Windows MSI. Linux currently needs the documented source/backend route. Developers need Bun, Rust, Python 3.12, just, Tauri prerequisites and Xcode on macOS; use just setup then just dev in the checkout.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/jamiepine/voicebox.git
```


```sh
cd voicebox
```


```sh
just setup
```


```sh
just dev
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/jamiepine/voicebox/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://voicebox.sh)

1. Install the matching release and download one chosen engine through model management.
2. Create a preset or authorized reference voice, select language and generate a short passage.
3. Compare the original with another take; assemble multiple voices in Stories and audition timing and effects.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/jamiepine/voicebox/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://voicebox.sh) [Source 3](https://github.com/jamiepine/voicebox/blob/main/docs/content/docs/overview/troubleshooting.mdx)

- **Hardware:** CPU inference is documented. LuxTTS claims roughly 1 GB VRAM for that engine; the troubleshooting guide recommends 6 GB+ VRAM generally, not a universal enforced minimum. Model downloads range approximately 350 MB to 8 GB per engine, not a complete installation total. System RAM and total-disk minima are not specified.
- **Software:** Packaged Tauri application; source development requires Python 3.12, Bun, Rust, just and platform Tauri dependencies. REST/MCP backend defaults to port 17493.
- **Platforms:** macOS Apple Silicon and Intel; Windows installer; Linux source/backend and Docker routes. Backend lists CUDA, ROCm, DirectML, Intel XPU and CPU with engine-specific differences.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/jamiepine/voicebox/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/jamiepine/voicebox/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://voicebox.sh)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** The MIT application license does not license Qwen, LuxTTS, Chatterbox, TADA, Kokoro or Whisper weights collectively. Review each selected engine/model card and reference-voice rights; models are separate downloads.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. The MIT application license does not license Qwen, LuxTTS, Chatterbox, TADA, Kokoro or Whisper weights collectively. Review each selected engine/model card and reference-voice rights; models are separate downloads.
- **Cost:** Application code is MIT; local hardware and model storage are required. Optional external clients and any selected service have separate terms.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/jamiepine/voicebox/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://voicebox.sh)

### Limitations

Only Chatterbox Turbo interprets the documented expressive tags; other engines may speak them literally. Windows/Linux automatic paste parity remains on the roadmap. A local UI does not make every model equally fast or prove every integration is private. [Source 1](https://github.com/jamiepine/voicebox/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://voicebox.sh)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/jamiepine/voicebox)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/jamiepine/voicebox/blob/main/README.md)

## Infinite Canvas

Images & design · Browser tools & web media · Interactive, immersive & live media · Video, animation & film

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Experimental

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Arrange references and prompt branches on a large canvas, compare generated visual directions and feed one result into the next iteration.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-12. No local execution implied.

Why Experimental: The current README explicitly warns of active development and potentially incompatible local storage/data-format changes affecting saved creative projects. https://github.com/basketikun/infinite-canvas/blob/main/README.md

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/basketikun/infinite-canvas/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The repository includes screenshots of the canvas and generated-image nodes. They document the interface; no independent output-quality comparison was established. [Source](https://github.com/basketikun/infinite-canvas/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Clone the source, enter web, install with Bun and start its development server; alternatively run the repository Docker Compose configuration and open localhost:3000. Configure your own compatible Base URL and API key in the UI. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/basketikun/infinite-canvas/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT covers the workspace. Selected hosted APIs, model weights, remote plugins and external prompt collections have their own terms and permissions. Workspace code is free under MIT; configured image/audio/video services may charge per generation. Sponsor offers are not validated prices. [Source](https://github.com/basketikun/infinite-canvas/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The maintainer explicitly warns that the project is in development and local storage formats may change without historical-data compatibility. Credentials and assets are browser-local by default, but generation requests go to the chosen provider.

### How it uses AI

A node-based browser workspace connects image, reference-editing, text, audio and video generation to user-configured OpenAI-compatible model endpoints, with an optional local agent/MCP bridge. [Source](https://github.com/basketikun/infinite-canvas/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A node-based browser workspace connects image, reference-editing, text, audio and video generation to user-configured OpenAI-compatible model endpoints, with an optional local agent/MCP bridge. [Source 1](https://github.com/basketikun/infinite-canvas/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Arrange references and prompt branches on a large canvas, compare generated visual directions and feed one result into the next iteration. [Source 1](https://github.com/basketikun/infinite-canvas/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The repository includes screenshots of the canvas and generated-image nodes. They document the interface; no independent output-quality comparison was established. [Source 1](https://github.com/basketikun/infinite-canvas/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Clone the source, enter web, install with Bun and start its development server; alternatively run the repository Docker Compose configuration and open localhost:3000. Configure your own compatible Base URL and API key in the UI. [Source 1](https://github.com/basketikun/infinite-canvas/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone the source, enter web, install with Bun and start its development server; alternatively run the repository Docker Compose configuration and open localhost:3000. Configure your own compatible Base URL and API key in the UI.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/basketikun/infinite-canvas.git
```


```sh
cd infinite-canvas/web
```


```sh
bun install
```


```sh
bun run dev
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/basketikun/infinite-canvas/blob/main/README.md)

1. Configure a model endpoint you are authorized to access.
2. Add a text prompt and image/reference nodes, connect the desired upstream material and generate a small example.
3. Compare branches, save/export the canvas and back up valuable assets before updating the application.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/basketikun/infinite-canvas/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Bun development environment and the React/Vite web application, or Docker Compose; modern browser and a compatible generation endpoint. Specific OS and browser version minima are not supplied.

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/1038lab/ComfyUI-QwenVL/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** The README suggests 16 GB+ VRAM for FP16, 8 GB+ for balanced 8-bit and lower-memory 4-bit options; these are rough model-dependent guidance, not guaranteed minima. RAM and disk minima are not specified.
- **Software:** Existing ComfyUI and its Python environment; Transformers/PyTorch or separately installed vision-capable llama-cpp-python. SageAttention is optional and GPU-specific.
- **Platforms:** ComfyUI host environment; advanced device controls list auto, CUDA and CPU. The reviewed README does not establish complete macOS/MPS or OS-version support.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/1038lab/ComfyUI-QwenVL/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/1038lab/ComfyUI-QwenVL/blob/main/README.md)

- **Weights:** GPL-3.0 software. Each Qwen or community fine-tune weight/projector license remains separate and must be checked for the exact selected model.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows GPL-3.0. GPL-3.0 software. Each Qwen or community fine-tune weight/projector license remains separate and must be checked for the exact selected model.

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/1038lab/ComfyUI-QwenVL/blob/main/README.md)

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/nsicart/MosaicEarth-Desktop/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

CPU processing is available and GPU support is checked per engine. Cloud catalog/basemaps still use the internet. Background terrain is not a survey-grade vertical datum, and a reconstructed scene is not automatically print-ready. [Source 1](https://github.com/nsicart/MosaicEarth-Desktop/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A self-hosted game-asset database includes a model-powered naming/tagging agent and MCP tools for storing, finding and reusing generated media with recipes and lineage. [Source 1](https://github.com/toriath/hoard/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Organize sprites, textures, sounds and models across game projects and let an assistant retrieve or describe the actual local asset inventory. [Source 1](https://github.com/toriath/hoard/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

### Install

Run the official Docker image with a persistent /data volume, open localhost:8080 and create the first account. Configure the AI provider in Settings and create an MCP token only for the clients that need it. [Source 1](https://github.com/toriath/hoard/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Run the official Docker image with a persistent /data volume, open localhost:8080 and create the first account. Configure the AI provider in Settings and create an MCP token only for the clients that need it.

```sh
docker run -d --name hoard -p 8080:8080 -v hoard-data:/data ghcr.io/toriath/hoard:latest
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/toriath/hoard/blob/main/README.md)

1. Import a small licensed asset pack and check its names, types and source rights.
2. Configure Claude or a suitable local OpenAI-compatible vision model for metadata analysis and inspect its proposed captions/tags.
3. Connect your agent over MCP, search the collection and record generation recipes and project usage.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/toriath/hoard/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Docker images for linux/amd64 and linux/arm64; standalone Linux/macOS binaries are also described. Source build requires Go 1.27+ and Node 24+.
- **Platforms:** Self-hosted Linux containers and documented Linux/macOS binaries; browser UI. A local model backend is a separate installation.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/toriath/hoard/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/toriath/hoard/blob/main/README.md)

- **Weights:** MIT server/UI. AI-model/provider terms and every stored asset license are separate. Pack-license detection is a heuristic and must not be treated as a legal grant.

These are maintainer reports, not independent reproduction. [Source](https://github.com/wpegley/imagineering-freecad-ai-bridge/blob/master/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Copy the versioned FCMacro into the FreeCAD macro directory and run it in the GUI. Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. A paused bridge still emits an apparently healthy monitoring heartbeat, and execute has no timeout to stop a runaway GUI-thread command. The textual guard is not a security boundary and run_macro is unsandboxed.

### How it uses AI

A FreeCAD macro provides a file-based command bridge, model inventory and embedded project memory so AI agents can edit a live parametric CAD document and resume its context later. [Source](https://github.com/wpegley/imagineering-freecad-ai-bridge/blob/master/README.md)

### Introduction

A FreeCAD macro provides a file-based command bridge, model inventory and embedded project memory so AI agents can edit a live parametric CAD document and resume its context later. [Source 1](https://github.com/wpegley/imagineering-freecad-ai-bridge/blob/master/README.md)

### What it is good for

Explore controlled CAD edits and agent-assisted inspection while keeping spreadsheet expressions, native document history and visual section captures available. [Source 1](https://github.com/wpegley/imagineering-freecad-ai-bridge/blob/master/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README reports tested inventory, memory save/reopen and second-process capture workflows on FreeCAD 26.3, with a later crash fix confirmed on 27.1. These are maintainer reports, not independent reproduction. [Source 1](https://github.com/wpegley/imagineering-freecad-ai-bridge/blob/master/README.md)

### Install

Copy the versioned FCMacro into the FreeCAD macro directory and run it in the GUI. Configure an agent to exchange the documented JSONL hello/execute messages and read the corresponding result; the macro itself needs no extra packages. [Source 1](https://github.com/wpegley/imagineering-freecad-ai-bridge/blob/master/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Copy the versioned FCMacro into the FreeCAD macro directory and run it in the GUI. Configure an agent to exchange the documented JSONL hello/execute messages and read the corresponding result; the macro itself needs no extra packages.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/wpegley/imagineering-freecad-ai-bridge/blob/master/README.md)

1. Back up a small native document and start the bridge in FreeCAD.
2. Register an agent, inspect spreadsheet aliases, expression bindings and the inventory, then request one bounded geometric change.
3. Read the actual result, inspect capture sections and checkpoint the embedded project log before saving.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/wpegley/imagineering-freecad-ai-bridge/blob/master/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** FreeCAD and its bundled Python; a client capable of file-based JSONL commands. Optional editor/voice integrations have their own dependencies.
- **Platforms:** Windows only today; the README explicitly says Linux/macOS have not been run. FreeCAD 26.3 is the stated initial test version, with a fix reported on 27.1.

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT macro. FreeCAD and optional separately run ScanRuler retain their own licenses; agent/model access is separate.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT macro. FreeCAD and optional separately run ScanRuler retain their own licenses; agent/model access is separate.
- **Cost:** Local CAD and agent compute; optional model-service access may cost money.

### Introduction

- **Platforms:** Apple Silicon M1 or later only; Intel Macs, Windows and Linux are not supported by this MLX workstation.

- **Cost:** Local GPU/CPU/storage; no required RunPod account for the default path. Optional cloud inference/storage and model permissions are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/skyphusion-labs/vivijure-local/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skyphusion-labs/vivijure-local/blob/main/USE.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/skyphusion-labs/vivijure-local/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_MODELS.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/skyphusion-labs/vivijure-local/releases/tag/v1.14.0)

### Limitations

With no GPU door the relevant controls are unavailable and no real film can render. The selected model/backend terms need direct review; upstream license guidance has dates and is not a blanket legal assurance. Single-operator trust model. The reviewed v1.14.0 release removes finish-binding fallbacks: a finish module must declare finish_artifacts; a module without it receives no R2 adoption and a rate factor of 1. [Source 1](https://github.com/skyphusion-labs/vivijure-local/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/skyphusion-labs/vivijure-local/blob/main/USE.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/skyphusion-labs/vivijure-local/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_MODELS.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/skyphusion-labs/vivijure-local/releases/tag/v1.14.0)

### Introduction

An agent-oriented SVG tool repairs diagram layout under a published machine-readable contract so AI-authored boxes, labels and edges can be checked and rearranged mechanically. [Source 1](https://github.com/takagiy/nicevg/blob/main/README.md)

2. Use Python 3.11+ and the documented environment/setup path, inspecting the third-party model dependencies first. Cloud streaming requires your own Modal GPU account; local/batch routes are separate. The README clone example still names Real-Eyes, so use the canonical Open-Reality repository.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/bdavidzhang/Open-Reality.git
```


```sh
cd Open-Reality
```


```sh
modal deploy modal_streaming.py
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/bdavidzhang/Open-Reality/blob/main/README.md)

1. Prepare an authorized short room walkthrough or image sequence and choose a local or explicit cloud route.
2. Inspect the reconstructed cameras/point cloud before asking the spatial agent to identify a visible object.
3. Compare detections and geometry with source frames; retain the capture as an uncertain visual reference rather than assuming dimensions or hidden objects are correct.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/bdavidzhang/Open-Reality/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Cloud streaming is documented on an H100, batch on an A100; the README cites roughly 4 GB VGGT weights plus other models, not a whole-stack requirement. Local RAM/VRAM/storage minima are not provided.
- **Software:** Python 3.11+, PyTorch/model dependencies and Vite/Three.js frontend; Modal account for cloud deployment, with Conda recommended in the guide.
- **Platforms:** Phone/browser capture frontend and separately provisioned Python/GPU backend. No complete native OS support matrix or local macOS GPU guarantee is given.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/bdavidzhang/Open-Reality/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/bdavidzhang/Open-Reality/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** BSD-2-Clause
- **Weights:** The complete BSD-2-Clause software grant was reviewed. VGGT, DINO-Salad, Perception Encoder, SAM3 and agent/provider components keep separate licenses; their full commercial-weight suitability was not established here.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows BSD-2-Clause. The complete BSD-2-Clause software grant was reviewed. VGGT, DINO-Salad, Perception Encoder, SAM3 and agent/provider components keep separate licenses; their full commercial-weight suitability was not established here.
- **Cost:** Modal GPU/runtime and external agent/model services may be billed; local alternatives still require substantial resources.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/bdavidzhang/Open-Reality/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Browser access does not eliminate heavy backend compute or cloud data transfer. The project’s emergency/inspection scenarios are proposals, not verified capabilities; the assessed scope is visual capture research only. [Source 1](https://github.com/bdavidzhang/Open-Reality/blob/main/README.md)

## Patchkamer

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Ask an external coding agent to design a small musical device, validate its interface and connect it to an inspectable live patch; existing synthesis is conventional DSP rather than a bundled neural model.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-12. No local execution implied.

The developer supplies songs and examples; independent musical-quality and agent-authoring reproduction were not established. The documented browser route has no build step. Device validation separately needs Node.js; no Node version minimum is specified. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. A valid device contract is not a listening-quality guarantee. AI-generated plug-ins are executable JavaScript and need review. The optional Stem Splitter sends audio to a separately paid service; the ordinary local modular studio does not require it.

### How it uses AI

A modular browser music studio includes a self-contained device authoring contract and validator expressly designed for people and AI agents to create playable synth and effect plug-ins. Place this repository under the documented hailo_apps/python/pipeline_apps tree, activate that infrastructure environment and run its camera application. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/sebastian-gh/pi-ki-musicvision/blob/main/README.md)

- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT project code. Hailo runtime, the selected pose-model weights, receiving synth and any samples retain their own terms. Pi, AI HAT+ 2, camera and an instrument/output device are separate hardware; no required cloud generation service is documented. [Source](https://github.com/sebastian-gh/pi-ki-musicvision/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The current project is an early work-in-progress proof of concept. Depth/stereo, multiple performers, visual projection and the planned 2027 performance are not the implemented Phase 1 workflow.

### How it uses AI

A Raspberry Pi camera pipeline runs a learned pose model on a Hailo AI accelerator and maps a performer’s hands to MIDI notes and continuous controls. [Source](https://github.com/sebastian-gh/pi-ki-musicvision/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A Raspberry Pi camera pipeline runs a learned pose model on a Hailo AI accelerator and maps a performer’s hands to MIDI notes and continuous controls. [Source 1](https://github.com/sebastian-gh/pi-ki-musicvision/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Prototype a body-controlled instrument or interactive sound installation using the current two-dimensional, single-person pose-to-MIDI workflow. [Source 1](https://github.com/sebastian-gh/pi-ki-musicvision/blob/main/README.md)

The README diagrams hand height as note pitch, palm movement as note triggering and left-hand height as CC1. Stereo depth, multi-person operation and the larger audiovisual performance are future phases, not demonstrated current features. [Source 1](https://github.com/sebastian-gh/pi-ki-musicvision/blob/main/README.md)

Prepare Raspberry Pi OS and the Hailo Apps infrastructure for AI HAT+ 2, plus python-rtmidi. Place this repository under the documented hailo_apps/python/pipeline_apps tree, activate that infrastructure environment and run its camera application. 3. Adjust height/range and note triggering in a small patch; account for occlusion and test latency on the actual installation hardware.

- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT project code. Hailo runtime, the selected pose-model weights, receiving synth and any samples retain their own terms.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/richer-richard/cochlea/blob/main/LICENSE-APACHE)

Local compute plus an independently chosen AI client; no required paid rendering service is documented. [Source](https://github.com/richer-richard/cochlea/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. MIDI/signal timing assertions do not prove musical interest. Pitch readback is scoped to monophonic tracks; heavy jitter may octave-fold detected BPM. Lossy MP3/OGG are analysis inputs, not original-sample recovery.

### How it uses AI

A headless Rust composition/rendering engine exposes compose, render, probe and verify operations through an MCP server so AI agents can write musical scores and inspect deterministic audio evidence. [Source](https://github.com/richer-richard/cochlea/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A headless Rust composition/rendering engine exposes compose, render, probe and verify operations through an MCP server so AI agents can write musical scores and inspect deterministic audio evidence. [Source 1](https://github.com/richer-richard/cochlea/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Build a short score, sting or synthetic sound cue under agent direction, then check note timing, loudness, rhythm and spectrograms before listening and editing. [Source 1](https://github.com/richer-richard/cochlea/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

Four worked demos include a metronome, chord pad, title cue and drum groove. The README publishes controlled signal-check results and known timing/tempo limits; those tests are not independent artistic evaluations. [Source 1](https://github.com/richer-richard/cochlea/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install the CLI and optional MCP server with Cargo, or use the source workspace. Configure the MCP executable in your chosen agent client. The documented stack decodes audio and writes WAV/PNG without FFmpeg, a GPU or an audio device. [Source 1](https://github.com/richer-richard/cochlea/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install the CLI and optional MCP server with Cargo, or use the source workspace. Configure the MCP executable in your chosen agent client. The documented stack decodes audio and writes WAV/PNG without FFmpeg, a GPU or an audio device.

```sh
cargo install cochlea
```


```sh
cargo install cochlea-mcp
```


```sh
cochlea render score.ron --verify
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/richer-richard/cochlea/blob/main/README.md)

1. Start with a demo score and have an AI client alter a small RON score using the documented tools.
2. Render a short cue, probe its stems and inspect the returned measurements and spectrogram.
3. Listen separately, revise musical choices and keep verification assertions tied to measurable properties rather than artistic success.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/richer-richard/cochlea/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** No GPU or audio device is required for headless processing. RAM, disk and CPU minima are not documented.
- **Software:** Rust/Cargo for installation; pure Rust decode/render dependencies and an external AI MCP client. No specific Rust version or tested OS matrix is supplied in the reviewed README.
- **Platforms:** Headless Rust CLI/server. macOS, Windows and Linux build parity is not asserted without documented platform evidence.

- **Weights:** The complete Apache-2.0 software grant was reviewed; MIT is an alternative. The AI assistant and any user audio or score assets are separate, with no neural weight bundled in the renderer.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. The complete Apache-2.0 software grant was reviewed; MIT is an alternative. The AI assistant and any user audio or score assets are separate, with no neural weight bundled in the renderer.

### How it uses AI

A Rust analysis CLI translates music into compact LRMD descriptors intended as perceptual context for language models, using deterministic DSP rather than a bundled neural listening model. [Source](https://github.com/Xuepoo/sonic-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A Rust analysis CLI translates music into compact LRMD descriptors intended as perceptual context for language models, using deterministic DSP rather than a bundled neural listening model. [Source 1](https://github.com/Xuepoo/sonic-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Give an AI assistant structured evidence about tempo, spectral balance and note/onset characteristics when discussing or revising a music track. [Source 1](https://github.com/Xuepoo/sonic-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README includes single-track output, optional onset analysis and two-file dynamic-time-warp comparison examples. Its speed and emotional-understanding claims have not received independent validation here. [Source 1](https://github.com/Xuepoo/sonic-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install sonic-bridge with Cargo, or choose the documented official release, Docker or Arch package route. Run it on a short permitted audio file and give the resulting descriptors to your chosen language model. [Source 1](https://github.com/Xuepoo/sonic-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install sonic-bridge with Cargo, or choose the documented official release, Docker or Arch package route. Run it on a short permitted audio file and give the resulting descriptors to your chosen language model.

```sh
cargo install sonic-bridge
```


```sh
sonic-bridge song.mp3 --onset
```

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Xuepoo/sonic-bridge/blob/main/README.md)

2. Ask your model to distinguish measured descriptors from subjective interpretation.
3. Compare another render using the documented two-input mode, and listen before drawing creative or mastering conclusions.

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/paul356/KiCad-AI-Assistant/blob/main/README.md)

1. Open a copy of a simple KiCad project and connect a selected model.
2. Ask for an explanation or a small reviewed schematic/layout edit, inspecting the resulting objects and diffs.
3. Run normal KiCad electrical/design-rule checks and specialist review; agent routing is not fabrication approval.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/paul356/KiCad-AI-Assistant/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** KiCad 10+, uv-managed plug-in Python environment and an Ollama or supported hosted model; optional freerouting Java tooling has separate setup requirements.
- **Platforms:** Linux and Windows are documented as tested. A macOS installation path is supplied, but that is not an independently validated macOS support statement.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/paul356/KiCad-AI-Assistant/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/paul356/KiCad-AI-Assistant/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT plug-in code. KiCad, model/service access, optional Java router and component/footprint data have their own licenses and requirements.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT plug-in code. KiCad, model/service access, optional Java router and component/footprint data have their own licenses and requirements.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/paul356/KiCad-AI-Assistant/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Model edits can damage a board or invent electronics. Work on a copy and review tool effects, ERC/DRC and actual component constraints; no current source establishes electrical or manufacturing certification. [Source 1](https://github.com/paul356/KiCad-AI-Assistant/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

A Python computational-optics toolkit includes actual PyTorch neural components for learned holographic light transport, including a spatially adaptive U-Net, alongside conventional wave simulation and hologram optimization. 1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/FridayBull/cutai.git
```


```sh
cd cutai
```


```sh
swiftc -O segment.swift -o segment
```


```sh
swiftc -O people.swift -o people
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/FridayBull/cutai/blob/main/README.md)

1. Scan your permitted clips and have an agent inspect their contact sheets and beat measurements.
2. Write a JSON edit plan and render one look frame per shot before the full movie.
3. Review the live before/after view and export MP4 or FCPXML for manual finishing.

A code-first 3D asset toolkit provides MCP/JSON inspection, CPU contact-sheet rendering, geometry checks and GLB building specifically for AI coding agents. [Source](https://github.com/MrsHorrid/stylemesh/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A code-first 3D asset toolkit provides MCP/JSON inspection, CPU contact-sheet rendering, geometry checks and GLB building specifically for AI coding agents. [Source 1](https://github.com/MrsHorrid/stylemesh/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Have an agent author a reusable prop once and compare several visual styles while keeping editable JavaScript asset definitions. [Source 1](https://github.com/MrsHorrid/stylemesh/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README shows four assets in six styles and ships thirty example assets. These are first-party illustrations, not independent engine import or performance validation. [Source 1](https://github.com/MrsHorrid/stylemesh/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Clone the source and install dependencies with Node 20+. Use the CLI examples, or register cli.js mcp as a stdio server for a compatible external AI agent. [Source 1](https://github.com/MrsHorrid/stylemesh/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone the source and install dependencies with Node 20+. Use the CLI examples, or register cli.js mcp as a stdio server for a compatible external AI agent.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/MrsHorrid/stylemesh
```


```sh
cd stylemesh
```


```sh
npm install
```


```sh
node cli.js render Prop_Boat --style painted
```


```sh
node cli.js build --style all
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/MrsHorrid/stylemesh/blob/main/README.md)

1. Start from a small example prop and ask the agent to edit a separate asset file.
2. Render the CPU contact sheet and inspect geometry, scale and style before building GLB.
3. Import the export in the actual target engine and check materials, normals and lighting there.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/MrsHorrid/stylemesh/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** CPU rendering without GPU/display is documented. RAM, storage and CPU minima are unspecified.
- **Software:** Node.js 20+, npm and an external coding/MCP agent. CPU previews require no GPU, browser or display; viewer mode is separate.
- **Platforms:** Node CLI workflow; the README does not establish a complete tested native OS/version matrix.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/MrsHorrid/stylemesh/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/MrsHorrid/stylemesh/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT toolkit and the supplied Reel Raiders examples. External agents, added source assets and target game engines retain their own terms; the renderer is conventional code, not a neural generator.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT toolkit and the supplied Reel Raiders examples. External agents, added source assets and target game engines retain their own terms; the renderer is conventional code, not a neural generator.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/MrsHorrid/stylemesh/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Some styles average materials to one color; Roblox ignores the documented vertex colors, normal maps ship without tangents and CPU previews use fixed lighting. Geometry checks do not establish final in-engine shading. [Source 1](https://github.com/MrsHorrid/stylemesh/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/MrsHorrid/stylemesh)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/MrsHorrid/stylemesh/blob/main/README.md)

The repository was created on 2026-11-10. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/JulianMunozCarrera/Rubato/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The developer supplies band-only and accompanied FLAC demos but explicitly says the guitar is synthesized. Evaluation uses held-out POP909 MIDI rendered by a simple synth; no real-recording or band-microphone validation is claimed. [Source](https://github.com/JulianMunozCarrera/Rubato/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Clone Rubato and install its requirements in a separate Python environment. Use play_along.py for a recorded-file experiment; live mode separately needs sounddevice, microphone input and headphones. Training is a different route with dataset/model dependencies. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/JulianMunozCarrera/Rubato/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.

- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT project grant reviewed. Dataset, trained model and performance/source-recording permissions remain separate; drums are rules rather than a neural generator. Local compute and permitted audio/model data; no mandatory cloud generation account is documented. [Source](https://github.com/JulianMunozCarrera/Rubato/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Only 4/4 is supported; slow tempos may double and chord changes can lag. The author reports correct bass harmony at the beat on only 59% of held-out synthesized-song cases. That known limitation does not establish live-stage readiness.

### How it uses AI

A music accompaniment system combines learned chord/bass models with real-time tempo tracking and rule-based drums to play along with incoming audio. [Source](https://github.com/JulianMunozCarrera/Rubato/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A music accompaniment system combines learned chord/bass models with real-time tempo tracking and rule-based drums to play along with incoming audio. [Source 1](https://github.com/JulianMunozCarrera/Rubato/blob/main/README.md)

Training is a different route with dataset/model dependencies. [Source 1](https://github.com/JulianMunozCarrera/Rubato/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

A dedicated AI-agent photo-book workflow combines optional Doubao image understanding and Seedream backgrounds with deterministic Pillow layouts that preserve the original photographs. [Source](https://github.com/1NWZ/memory-zine-book/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A dedicated AI-agent photo-book workflow combines optional Doubao image understanding and Seedream backgrounds with deterministic Pillow layouts that preserve the original photographs. [Source 1](https://github.com/1NWZ/memory-zine-book/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Arrange one to nine permission-cleared photographs into a short static memory zine, using model suggestions only where desired and approving the actual crops and narrative. [Source 1](https://github.com/1NWZ/memory-zine-book/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README describes fixed cover/spread layouts and a default 1600×2133 PNG output. Third-party reference images were removed from the public package; the layout can still render without them. [Source 1](https://github.com/1NWZ/memory-zine-book/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Clone the complete source, create a Python 3.10+ environment and install requirements. A compatible agent can use its workflow, or the local render_book.py helper can create, validate and render a plan. Configure remote AI separately through private model settings and environment credentials. [Source 1](https://github.com/1NWZ/memory-zine-book/blob/main/README.md)

2. Clone the complete source, create a Python 3.10+ environment and install requirements. A compatible agent can use its workflow, or the local render_book.py helper can create, validate and render a plan. Configure remote AI separately through private model settings and environment credentials.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/1NWZ/memory-zine-book.git
```


```sh
cd memory-zine-book
```


```sh
python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
python scripts/render_book.py plan /absolute/photo1.jpg /absolute/photo2.jpg --out work/book-plan.json
```


```sh
python scripts/render_book.py validate work/book-plan.json
```


```sh
python scripts/render_book.py render work/book-plan.json --out work/book-v1
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/1NWZ/memory-zine-book/blob/main/README.md)

1. Prepare an empty work directory and a plan using your own photos.
2. Review crop, subject and chronology; optional model analysis produces suggestions that must be merged into the documented plan schema.
3. Validate and render static pages, inspect all original-photo placement and text, and retain the source files and plan as a backup.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/1NWZ/memory-zine-book/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python 3.10+, Pillow/requirements and an available Chinese font for Chinese type; a separate coding agent or optional Ark model access for AI stages.

### Limitations

Output is static PNG, not a page-turn video, print imposition or printer-certified book. AI analysis sends downscaled EXIF-stripped photos to the selected endpoint; the local renderer alone does not. Analysis JSON is not itself a valid render plan. [Source 1](https://github.com/1NWZ/memory-zine-book/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

A ComfyUI node pack offers a dedicated plate-editing page where neural image edits, video propagation and learned masks carry painted changes through a shot, with OpenColorIO/EXR-aware review. [Source](https://github.com/AInVFX/ainvfx-paintaplate/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A ComfyUI node pack offers a dedicated plate-editing page where neural image edits, video propagation and learned masks carry painted changes through a shot, with OpenColorIO/EXR-aware review. [Source 1](https://github.com/AInVFX/ainvfx-paintaplate/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Prototype erasure, painted effects, relighting or set-extension changes while comparing them with the original plate and preserving a traceable workflow. [Source 1](https://github.com/AInVFX/ainvfx-paintaplate/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The developer links a four-minute showcase and a longer interface demo with first-frame edits carried through shots. Award claims and synthetic test plates are not independent production-quality proof. [Source 1](https://github.com/AInVFX/ainvfx-paintaplate/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install the pack and ComfyUI-LTXVideo through ComfyUI Manager or clone both into custom_nodes and install their requirements with the host Python. Obtain the separately gated models, restart and open the pack’s paintaplate.html page. Mac/smaller-GPU users are directed to a remote NVIDIA route. [Source 1](https://github.com/AInVFX/ainvfx-paintaplate/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install the pack and ComfyUI-LTXVideo through ComfyUI Manager or clone both into custom_nodes and install their requirements with the host Python. Obtain the separately gated models, restart and open the pack’s paintaplate.html page. Mac/smaller-GPU users are directed to a remote NVIDIA route.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/AInVFX/ainvfx-paintaplate
```


```sh
git clone https://github.com/Lightricks/ComfyUI-LTXVideo
```


```sh
python -m pip install -r ainvfx-paintaplate/requirements.txt -r ComfyUI-LTXVideo/requirements.txt
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/AInVFX/ainvfx-paintaplate/blob/main/README.md)

1. Use a short permission-cleared plate and inspect its color/EXR interpretation.
2. Edit and mask the first frame, then run a brief neural propagation and compare full-frame results through the color-managed viewer.
3. Check all changed and supposedly untouched regions, temporal continuity, audio and output metadata before handing the result to a production compositor.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/AInVFX/ainvfx-paintaplate/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** The author recommends/tests 32 GB+ VRAM, with 96 GB for current above-2K plate workloads; these are workflow-specific reports. Models total about 60 GB; a 150 GB cloud container is an example, not a universal local minimum. System RAM minima are not specified.
- **Software:** ComfyUI 0.43.0+, ComfyUI-LTXVideo, Python dependencies including OpenEXR/OpenColorIO and the specified image/video/segmentation model files.
- **Platforms:** Pack installation is documented on Windows/macOS/Linux, but the neural models require a large NVIDIA backend. A browser on a Mac is not evidence of local MPS inference support.

FLUX, LTX/IC-LoRA, SAM and prompt/text-encoder resources retain their own licenses, gated access and possible commercial conditions; the synthetic test plates are CC BY 4.0. [Source](https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/PanoramaGenInpaint/blob/feat/top_bottom_refine/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Multiple successive inpainting passes take minutes according to the author. The result is a 360° image, not measured geometry, a stereo depth map or automatically comfortable VR content.

### How it uses AI

A training-free neural inpainting pipeline builds an equirectangular 360° image by projecting and progressively synthesizing overlapping perspective regions. [Source](https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/PanoramaGenInpaint/blob/feat/top_bottom_refine/README.md)

### Introduction

A training-free neural inpainting pipeline builds an equirectangular 360° image by projecting and progressively synthesizing overlapping perspective regions. [Source 1](https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/PanoramaGenInpaint/blob/feat/top_bottom_refine/README.md)

### What it is good for

Develop panoramic environment references from a scene idea, reviewing horizons, seams and semantic continuity before immersive use. [Source 1](https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/PanoramaGenInpaint/blob/feat/top_bottom_refine/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README shows a market-square and Japanese-garden panorama plus an anchored projection diagram. These are author examples, not independent seam, stereo or headset-quality tests. [Source 1](https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/PanoramaGenInpaint/blob/feat/top_bottom_refine/README.md)

### Install

Clone the source, create its documented Python 3.10 Conda environment and install requirements. Supply access and permission for the actual FLUX-Dev/ControlNet model route, then run world.py with scene, side and sky prompts. [Source 1](https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/PanoramaGenInpaint/blob/feat/top_bottom_refine/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone the source, create its documented Python 3.10 Conda environment and install requirements. Supply access and permission for the actual FLUX-Dev/ControlNet model route, then run world.py with scene, side and sky prompts.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/PanoramaGenInpaint.git
```


```sh
cd PanoramaGenInpaint
```


```sh
conda create -n pano_gen python=3.10
```


```sh
conda activate pano_gen
```


```sh
pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
python world.py --scene_prompt "a garden" --scene_prompt_sides "a garden" --sky_prompt "a clear sky"
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/PanoramaGenInpaint/blob/feat/top_bottom_refine/README.md)

1. Start with one small scene concept and explicitly choose the permitted model resources.
2. Inspect imgs/pano.png in an equirectangular viewer, checking the wrap seam, sky/ground and repeated subjects.
3. Use debug masks to understand failures and keep the result as a panoramic reference rather than claiming a reconstructed 3D world.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/PanoramaGenInpaint/blob/feat/top_bottom_refine/README.md)

- **Software:** Conda/Python 3.10, the repository requirements and FLUX-ControlNet inpainting dependencies; exact GPU/CUDA and OS version minima are not documented.
- **Platforms:** Python model-inference workflow. Native Windows/macOS/Linux support and local Mac inference are not established by these instructions.

- **Weights:** MIT pipeline code. The referenced FLUX-Dev and ControlNet code/weights have separate terms, which may restrict particular commercial uses; the paper’s availability is not a universal model grant.
